Choosing the\ncurrent dialect avoids a re-pin when downstream consumers (e.g. IDE\n`yaml-language-server`) update.\n\n## Top-level shape (DM-011)\n\n```\ntype: object\nadditionalProperties: false   # AC: \"forbids unknown required keys\"\nrequired: [name, version, description, defaults,\n           required_binaries, optional_capabilities, evals]\n```\n\n| Field | Type | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `name` | string (minLength 1) | Suite name; matches CLI `--suite`. |\n| `version` | string (pattern `^[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?$`) | Schema-version field per M1 Risk-3 forward-evolution. |\n| `description` | string | Human-readable summary. |\n| `defaults` | object (additionalProperties true) | Per-eval default knobs. Named keys: `per_eval_timeout_sec` (int≥1), `per_eval_memory_mb` (int≥1), `capture_tty` (bool), `keep_home_on_success` (bool). |\n| `required_binaries` | array of `requiredBinary` | HARD capability gates. |\n| `optional_capabilities` | array of `optionalCapability` | SOFT capability gates. |\n| `evals` | array of `evalEntry` | Eval entries (DM-002 shape). |\n\n## `$defs` block\n\n| `$def` | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `failureMode` | enum `[hard, skip, xfail]` (DM-007). |\n| `evalIdString` | string with FR-SCH2 regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. |\n| `requiredBinary` | `{name, min_version?, failure_mode}` — `additionalProperties: false`. |\n| `optionalCapability` | `{name, gate_flag?, failure_mode}` — `additionalProperties: false`. |\n| `isolationBlock` | `{home_strategy in [ephemeral, seeded, shared], seed_state[]}`. |\n| `parameterizeRow` | object with `minProperties: 1`, `propertyNames pattern ^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`, values constrained to `string|number|boolean`. |\n| `evalEntry` | DM-002 entry. `additionalProperties: false`. Required keys: `id, title`. |\n\n## `evals[]` entry shape (DM-002)\n\n`evalEntry` declares all 9 DM-002 fields:\n\n| Field | Type |\n|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` (FR-SCH2 regex) |\n| `title` | string (minLength 1) |\n| `category` | string |\n| `requires` | array of strings |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer (≥1) |\n| `isolation` | `isolationBlock` |\n| `inputs` | array of objects |\n| `expects` | array of objects |\n| `parameterize` | array of `parameterizeRow` (minItems 1) |\n\nOnly `id` and `title` are schema-required; remaining fields are optional so\nauthors can compose minimal entries.\n\n## `parameterize` shape\n\n`parameterize` is an **array of substitution rows**. Each row is an object\nof `placeholder -> value` pairs where:\n\n* Placeholder names match `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$` (Python-identifier\n  style, so `{{prefix}}` and `{{tool}}` substitutions are unambiguous).\n* Values are scalars (`string|number|boolean`) — complex structures are\n  rejected to keep template expansion deterministic.\n* The row must declare at least one key (`minProperties: 1`).\n* The schema validates row shape only; **semantic** expansion (E2 →\n  `E2.1, E2.2, ...`) and post-expansion id re-validation against FR-SCH2\n  happen in COMP-002 (T01.07).\n\nTemplate tokens inside `id:` are blocked at this layer because the\n`evalIdString` regex disallows `{` and `}`; T01.05 re-applies the same\nregex programmatically post-expansion.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.02) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| File `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/suite.schema.json` exists and is jsonschema-valid against a documented dialect. | `suite.schema.json` declares `$schema = draft/2020-12`; `Draft202012Validator.check_schema()` passes. |\n| Schema declares required `name,version,description,defaults,required_binaries,optional_capabilities,evals` and forbids unknown required keys. | Top-level `required: [...]` + `additionalProperties: false`. |\n| Reference fixture validates green. | `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/valid_suite.yaml` + `test_reference_manifest_validates_green`. |\n| Fixture missing a required field is rejected. | `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/missing_name_suite.yaml` + `test_missing_required_field_is_rejected`. |\n| spec.md documents schema field rules and `parameterize` shape. | This document. |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- **COMP-002 SuiteLoader (T01.07)** — loads YAML manifests and validates\n  them against this schema before any `validate_eval_id` invocation.\n- **FR-SCH1 `validate_manifest` (T01.04)** — exposes the schema check as\n  a typed function returning `list[EvalSpec]` or raising `SchemaError`.\n- **FR-CLI4 `eval doctor` (T01.13)** — surfaces schema violations as\n  exit-2 with the offending JSON path.\n\n## Notes / deferred work\n\n- Allow-list `additionalProperties: false` at the top level. Inside\n  `defaults` we keep `additionalProperties: true` because suite authors\n  may extend the per-eval defaults shape per Phase 4 needs without\n  schema churn; the named DM-011 keys are still type-checked.\n- `evalEntry.additionalProperties` is `false` to keep the DM-002 contract\n  closed; adding new fields requires bumping the schema `version` per\n  M1 Risk-3.\n- The schema is intentionally light on **semantic** validation\n  (parameterize-expansion safety, capability-name resolution); those\n  remain runtime checks in COMP-002 (T01.07) and CapabilityGates (T01.11).\n","numLines":116,"startLine":1,"totalLines":116}}}

Read the manifest from disk as raw bytes and decode YAML.\n2. Validate the decoded mapping against ``suite.schema.json`` (DM-011,\n   T01.02) using ``jsonschema``'s Draft 2020-12 validator.\n3. On any violation raise :class:`SchemaError` with a stable field-path\n   message; the CLI catches it and exits with :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`\n   (= 2 per the harness exit-code table in design-spec §4).\n4. On success, project every ``evals[]`` entry through\n   ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` and return the resulting list.\n\nWhat this module is **not** responsible for:\n\n* FR-SCH2 eval-id regex enforcement — lives in :func:`validate_eval_id`\n  (T01.05) and is applied by the SuiteLoader orchestrator (T01.07) both\n  pre- and post-parameterize-expansion.\n* Capability resolution and parameterize expansion — also COMP-002 (T01.07).\n* Any filesystem write (artifact dirs, scratch HOMEs, log files).\n  Callers MUST treat ``validate_manifest`` as a pure read so that schema\n  rejections cannot leak partial state onto disk (NFR-SEC1 invariant).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Iterable, Mapping\n\nimport yaml\nfrom jsonschema import Draft202012Validator\nfrom jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError\n\nfrom .models import EvalSpec\nfrom .suites import SCHEMA_PATH\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"SchemaError\",\n    \"SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE\",\n    \"validate_manifest\",\n]\n\n\nSCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`SchemaError` reaches the CLI boundary.\n\nMatches the design-spec §4 exit-code table: ``2`` = \"Harness error\n(manifest invalid, claude binary missing, etc.)\". The constant is exported\nso CLI callers and tests can assert against a single source of truth\nwithout duplicating the literal.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _Violation:\n    \"\"\"One jsonschema error projected into a stable, log-friendly shape.\"\"\"\n\n    path: str\n    message: str\n\n    def render(self) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.path}: {self.message}\"\n\n\nclass SchemaError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a manifest fails ``suite.schema.json`` validation.\n\n    The exception carries the manifest path, every collected\n    :class:`jsonschema.exceptions.ValidationError` projected into a\n    field-path / message pair, and a pre-rendered multi-line ``message``\n    suitable for stderr. The CLI maps this to exit code\n    :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        manifest_path: Filesystem path of the manifest that failed.\n        violations: Tuple of ``(json_path, error_message)`` pairs, one per\n            jsonschema error. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        manifest_path: Path,\n        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n        )\n        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n\n\ndef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n\n    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not error.absolute_path:\n        return \"$\"\n    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n        if isinstance(segment, int):\n            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n        else:\n            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n\n    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n    except OSError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    try:\n        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n        violation = _Violation(\n            path=\"$\",\n            message=(\n                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n            ),\n        )\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n\n    return decoded\n\n\ndef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n        in the manifest. Order matches the manifest order so downstream\n        report ordering is deterministic.\n\n    Raises:\n        SchemaError: Raised when the manifest cannot be read, cannot be\n            decoded as YAML, decodes to a non-mapping, or violates any\n            constraint in ``suite.schema.json``. The exception carries\n            one violation entry per jsonschema error, each annotated\n            with a ``$.path.to.field`` JSON path. CLI callers MUST map\n            this to :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = Path(path)\n    manifest = _read_manifest(manifest_path)\n\n    schema = _load_schema()\n    validator = Draft202012Validator(schema)\n    errors = sorted(\n        validator.iter_errors(manifest),\n        key=lambda e: (list(e.absolute_path), e.message),\n    )\n    if errors:\n        violations = [\n            _Violation(path=_format_json_path(err), message=err.message)\n            for err in errors\n        ]\n        raise SchemaError(manifest_path, violations)\n\n    return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry) for entry in manifest[\"evals\"]]\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":551,"cache_read_input_tokens":113993,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":551,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":56,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6fbda1c7-d1c2-4eec-84e7-510babba2caf","uuid":"a49728c1-1da0-44a5-aa8d-a784d0c7d2de"}

No scratch dirs or\n   artifact paths are touched.\n2. **Decode** YAML with `yaml.safe_load`. Decode errors are wrapped in\n   `SchemaError` with path `$`.\n3. **Type-check root** — manifest must decode to a mapping; scalars and\n   lists raise `SchemaError`.\n4. **Validate** against `suite.schema.json` using\n   `jsonschema.Draft202012Validator`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_0115ygQgx8ocWnF3eyVktv1F","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01DDDrmTUSQy8D8sP4arUWEV","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0004/notes.md","content":"# D-0004 — implementation notes\n\n## Decisions made during build\n\n1. **`SchemaError` collects every violation, not just the first.**\n   `jsonschema` returns one error per failure via `iter_errors`; the\n   default behaviour of `validate(...)` raises only the first. We\n   collect all errors so a single CLI run surfaces every shape problem\n   to the manifest author instead of forcing a fix → re-run → next-error\n   loop. Sorting on `(absolute_path, message)` keeps the list stable.\n\n2. **JSON-path rendering uses `$`-rooted dot/bracket notation.** This\n   matches the convention used by `jq -e`, `jsonpath`, and the\n   `mcp-eval` reference port. `$` is reserved for top-level missing\n   required fields where jsonschema leaves `absolute_path` empty —\n   without `$`, those errors would render as the empty string and\n   become unidentifiable in stderr.\n\n3. **`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE = 2` is a module-level constant.** The CLI\n   boundary (T01.13 `eval doctor`, M2 `eval run`) imports the symbol\n   instead of duplicating the literal. This keeps the design-spec §4\n   exit-code table and the implementation in lockstep.\n\n4. **YAML decode + missing-file + non-mapping all map to `SchemaError`.**\n   CLI callers only need to catch one typed error to honour FR-SCH1\n   (\"exits 2 before any FS write\"). `FileNotFoundError`, `OSError`, and\n   `yaml.YAMLError` would otherwise leak out as generic exceptions and\n   force every caller to maintain its own translation layer.\n\n5. **Schema is loaded via `yaml.safe_load`.** The schema file is\n   JSON-formatted but `yaml.safe_load` accepts JSON as a strict subset\n   of YAML and avoids pulling in an additional import path. The\n   alternative (`json.loads`) would force a second decoder for no\n   benefit.\n\n6. **`Path.read_text` chosen over `open(...)` context managers.**\n   Makes the FR-SCH1 invariant (\"no filesystem writes before\n   validation succeeds\") trivially auditable — every IO line in\n   `loader.py` reads from a `Path`; the absence of `Path.write_text`,\n   `os.mkdir`, etc. is verifiable by grep.\n\n7. **`EvalSpec` projection happens AFTER validation passes.** This\n   keeps the function's pre-condition trivially provable: by the time\n   we hit `EvalSpec.from_dict`, the manifest is schema-valid, so the\n   factory's `id`/`title` requirement cannot fail. Any `ValueError`\n   from the factory would indicate a contract bug rather than user\n   error.\n\n8. **Test fixture `invalid_eval_entry_suite.yaml` added.** The\n   T01.02 fixtures only cover top-level violations; we needed a\n   manifest with a violation *nested inside* `evals[]` to verify the\n   `$.evals[0].id` JSON-path rendering. The fixture deliberately\n   violates two rules at once (missing `title`, lower-case id) so the\n   determinism test has a non-trivial multi-violation case to compare.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.04\n\n- Runtime `validate_eval_id(eval_id)` — T01.05 (FR-SCH2). The schema\n  enforces the regex on *static* ids; post-parameterize-expanded ids\n  still need the runtime guard, which T01.07 wires.\n- CLI wiring (`eval doctor`, `eval run`) — T01.13 / M2.\n- Capability resolution / parameterize expansion — T01.07 (SuiteLoader).\n- Scratch-root allowlist enforcement — T01.19 (AC12).\n\n## Risks / follow-ups\n\n- The `_format_json_path` helper hand-rolls a JSON-pointer-ish notation;\n  if downstream tooling later expects RFC 6901 JSON Pointers we will\n  need to translate. Keeping the helper isolated makes that swap a\n  one-function change.\n- `SchemaError.violations` is currently a tuple of plain `(path, msg)`\n  pairs. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DDDrmTUSQy8D8sP4arUWEV","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0004/notes.md (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6fbda1c7-d1c2-4eec-84e7-510babba2caf","uuid":"b7541637-fb96-4e03-8379-951a02aaeab3","timestamp":"2026-05-20T05:00:26.739Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0004/notes.md","content":"# D-0004 — implementation notes\n\n## Decisions made during build\n\n1. **`SchemaError` collects every violation, not just the first.**\n   `jsonschema` returns one error per failure via `iter_errors`; the\n   default behaviour of `validate(...)` raises only the first. We\n   collect all errors so a single CLI run surfaces every shape problem\n   to the manifest author instead of forcing a fix → re-run → next-error\n   loop. Sorting on `(absolute_path, message)` keeps the list stable.\n\n2. **JSON-path rendering uses `$`-rooted dot/bracket notation.** This\n   matches the convention used by `jq -e`, `jsonpath`, and the\n   `mcp-eval` reference port. `$` is reserved for top-level missing\n   required fields where jsonschema leaves `absolute_path` empty —\n   without `$`, those errors would render as the empty string and\n   become unidentifiable in stderr.\n\n3. **`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE = 2` is a module-level constant.** The CLI\n   boundary (T01.13 `eval doctor`, M2 `eval run`) imports the symbol\n   instead of duplicating the literal. This keeps the design-spec §4\n   exit-code table and the implementation in lockstep.\n\n4. **YAML decode + missing-file + non-mapping all map to `SchemaError`.**\n   CLI callers only need to catch one typed error to honour FR-SCH1\n   (\"exits 2 before any FS write\"). `FileNotFoundError`, `OSError`, and\n   `yaml.YAMLError` would otherwise leak out as generic exceptions and\n   force every caller to maintain its own translation layer.\n\n5. **Schema is loaded via `yaml.safe_load`.** The schema file is\n   JSON-formatted but `yaml.safe_load` accepts JSON as a strict subset\n   of YAML and avoids pulling in an additional import path. The\n   alternative (`json.loads`) would force a second decoder for no\n   benefit.\n\n6. **`Path.read_text` chosen over `open(...)` context managers.**\n   Makes the FR-SCH1 invariant (\"no filesystem writes before\n   validation succeeds\") trivially auditable — every IO line in\n   `loader.py` reads from a `Path`; the absence of `Path.write_text`,\n   `os.mkdir`, etc. is verifiable by grep.\n\n7. **`EvalSpec` projection happens AFTER validation passes.** This\n   keeps the function's pre-condition trivially provable: by the time\n   we hit `EvalSpec.from_dict`, the manifest is schema-valid, so the\n   factory's `id`/`title` requirement cannot fail. Any `ValueError`\n   from the factory would indicate a contract bug rather than user\n   error.\n\n8. **Test fixture `invalid_eval_entry_suite.yaml` added.** The\n   T01.02 fixtures only cover top-level violations; we needed a\n   manifest with a violation *nested inside* `evals[]` to verify the\n   `$.evals[0].id` JSON-path rendering. The fixture deliberately\n   violates two rules at once (missing `title`, lower-case id) so the\n   determinism test has a non-trivial multi-violation case to compare.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.04\n\n- Runtime `validate_eval_id(eval_id)` — T01.05 (FR-SCH2).
